## S1 lessons the executor must honor

1. **Before declaring any new script/CLI complete**: actually invoke it with `--help` (or equivalent entry point) to confirm it parses and basic argparse setup works. Pytest passing is necessary but not sufficient — pytest may bypass `main()`.
2. **Any SQL fragment in a Python string**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` against the live schema, or sanity-check the aggregate/operator types against actual column types (e.g. `max(uuid)` doesn't exist in Postgres).
3. **Any asyncpg connection** against the configured `DATABASE_URL`: pass `statement_cache_size=0` — Supabase transaction-mode pooler (port 6543) is what's configured. Without this flag, scripts crash on first prepared-statement reuse.
4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs**: Python's `hash()` is salted per-process. Use `hashlib.sha1` for advisory-lock-id derivation or anything needing cross-run determinism.
5. **Schema filters in validation queries**: production tables live in schema `mediator`, NOT `public`. `information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema = 'mediator'`.

- Any artifact INSERT path can't fit `artifact_topics` write in the same transaction — STOP, report.

## Phase 1: Wire routing into inbound; surface scope on TurnContext

### Step 3: Promote `_decode_discord_user_id` to a shared module (`app/services/discord_id.py`, `scripts/seed_channels.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `app/services/discord_id.py`:
   ```python
   import base64, os, re

   _DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_RE = re.compile(r"^\d+$")

   def _decode_discord_user_id(token: str | None) -> str | None:
       if not token:
           return None
       try:
           segment = token.split(".", 1)[0]
           padded = segment + "=" * (-len(segment) % 4)
           decoded = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(padded).decode()
           return decoded if _DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_RE.match(decoded) else None
       except (ValueError, UnicodeDecodeError):
           return None

   def discord_bot_user_id() -> str | None:
       explicit = os.environ.get("DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID")
       if explicit and _DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_RE.match(explicit):
           return explicit
       return _decode_discord_user_id(os.environ.get("DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN"))
   ```
2. **Update** `scripts/seed_channels.py:55-90` to `from app.services.discord_id import _decode_discord_user_id`. Single source of truth; the seeder behavior is unchanged.
3. **No changes** to `app/config.py` Settings (avoids adding a field that would ripple through Pydantic validators).

### Step 4: Async startup hook for the relationship topic id (`app/bots/registry.py`, `app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `populate_topic_ids_from_db(pool)` to `app/bots/registry.py`. Stores the UUID on a **module-level slot ONLY** — no `MEDIATOR_BOT` mutation, no `BotSpec` field:
   ```python
   _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID: UUID | None = None

   async def populate_topic_ids_from_db(pool):
       global _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID
       row = await pool.fetchrow("SELECT id FROM mediator.topics WHERE slug = 'relationship'")
       _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID = row["id"] if row else None

   def get_relationship_topic_id() -> UUID | None:
       return _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID
   ```

> **Edit-safety contract for `write_tools.py`** (dirty file, INSERT-only carve-out):
> - Every change uses the `Edit` tool with a small unique `old_string` matching only the affected SQL fragment.
> - **Do not** delete or re-flow surrounding code, comments, or whitespace.
> - **Each `write_tools.py` change lands in its own commit** so accidental reflow is caught at review time.
> - After every edit, run `git diff --stat app/services/tools/write_tools.py` and confirm only additive changes appear at the targeted line range.
> - For existing multi-arm CTEs (`supersede_memory`, `revise_distillation`), **ADD a new arm** — never rewrite.

1. **Simple artifact INSERTs** (memories `:699`, themes `:764`, watch_items `:800`, observations `:878`, distillations `:939`, out_of_bounds `:1109`) rewrite from:

   ```sql
   WITH new_artifact AS (
       INSERT INTO memories (..., recorded_by_bot_id) VALUES (..., $K) RETURNING id
   ), topic_link AS (
       INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status)
       SELECT 'memories', new_artifact.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new_artifact
   )
   SELECT id FROM new_artifact
   ```
   `recorded_by_bot_id = ctx.bot_id`, `topic_id = ctx.primary_topic_id`.
2. **`supersede_memory` (`:737-757`)** — existing multi-arm CTE. **ADD** a third arm `, topic_link AS (INSERT INTO artifact_topics ... SELECT 'memories', new.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new)`. Keep `WITH old AS (UPDATE memories ...)` verbatim.
3. **`revise_distillation` (`:1041-1092`)** — existing multi-arm CTE (`old`, `new`, `revised_old`). **ADD** a fourth arm `, topic_link AS (INSERT INTO artifact_topics ... SELECT 'distillations', new.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new)`. Keep `revised_old` and the trailing `JOIN`/`SELECT`.
4. **STOP** and report if any artifact INSERT can't fit `artifact_topics` in the same statement. `_schedule_context_job` follow-ups on `watch_items`/`out_of_bounds` are intentionally separate (artifact + topic link atomic; scheduled-job side-effect outside) — DO NOT trigger STOP.

### Step 10: Bridge candidate + feedback inserts; withheld_reviews kwarg pass-through (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`, `app/services/discord.py`, `app/services/withheld_reviews.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **bridge_candidates** INSERT at `write_tools.py:305-329`:
   - `bot_id = ctx.bot_id`
   - `topic_id = ctx.primary_topic_id`
   - `dyad_id = ctx.dyad_id` (NOT `ctx.binding_id` — `bot_bindings.id` vs `bot_bindings.dyad_id` are distinct).
   Use the Step 9 edit-safety contract.
2. **Feedback INSERTs:**
   - `write_tools.py:1763` (`log_feedback`): `bot_id=ctx.bot_id`, `topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`.
   - `discord.py:468-478` reaction handler: resolve scope via the **bot's** address (matches `channels.address` per `scripts/seed_channels.py:95-101`):
     ```python
     from app.services.discord_id import discord_bot_user_id
     bot_user_id = discord_bot_user_id()
     scope = await _resolve_scope(self.pool, 'discord', bot_user_id) if bot_user_id else None
     bot_id = scope.bot_id if scope else 'mediator'
     topic_id = scope.topic_id if scope else get_relationship_topic_id()
     ```
     Use the resolved values in the feedback INSERT. Never the reacting user's id.
3. **withheld_reviews row INSERTs** (`record_withheld_outbound_review` in `app/services/withheld_reviews.py`): **thread `bot_id`/`topic_id` through the function signature as new optional kwargs**, but do NOT change the SQL column list in S2a — the table has no scope columns yet. Callers (`messaging.py:207-216, 342-365`) pass them. This unblocks S2b's migration + lint-rule extension. Document in `s2a_preflight.md`.

## Phase 3: Dual-key rekey + per-(user,bot) pause read paths

### Step 11: Dual-key BurstCoalescer (`app/services/debouncer.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** `_Burst` with optional `bot_id: str | None = None`.
2. **Rekey** internal dicts to composite tuple keys `(user_id, bot_id_or_None)`. Lookups try the composite first, fall back to `(user_id, None)` (locked decision #1). Internal `AsyncBurstCoalescer` (third-party) remains keyed by `user_id`; bot-aware dispatch happens at `_fire_batch`.
3. **Add** `bot_id` kwarg to `BurstCoalescer.add(...)` (default `None`), propagate to `_Burst`.
4. **Mark** each fallback site with `# TODO(S2b): drop legacy (user_id, None) fallback`.

### Step 12: Dual-key newer-inbound suppression (`app/services/agentic.py`, `app/services/tools/read_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Modify** `_newer_inbound_exists` at `agentic.py:592-625`: add `AND (bot_id = $4 OR bot_id IS NULL)` to the EXISTS query, bind `ctx.bot_id` as a new parameter.
2. **Same** at `read_tools.py:152-179`.

### Step 13: Per-(user, bot) pause read path — broader survey + gateway audit (`app/services/system_state.py`, `messaging.py`, `scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `oob_check.py`, `withheld_reviews.py`, `recovery.py`, `pacer.py`, `discord.py`, `whatsapp.py`, `hooks.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `user_bot_paused(pool, user_id, bot_id) -> bool` in `system_state.py` (reads `user_bot_state.paused`).
2. **Add** an optional `bot_id` kwarg to `hooks.paused_for_user` (`app/services/hooks.py:37`). Callers in `messaging.py:170,335` pass `ctx.bot_id`. Internal implementation may treat `None` as global pause only — but having the parameter is the readiness step for S2b.
3. **Gate sites:**
   - `messaging.py:170` (`send_outbound_part`), `:335` (`send_outbound`): withhold if `is_paused OR user_bot_paused(pool, user.id, bot_id)`.
   - `scheduled_job_handlers.py` dispatch (`scheduled_jobs.py:134`-side or in each handler's entry): gate before fire.
   - `oob_check.py`: user-visible release.
   - `withheld_reviews.py`: released-review emissions.
   - `recovery.py:86,114`: re-injects via `coalescer.add` / `add_burst`; downstream turn goes through `send_outbound` → gated centrally. Annotate with `# pause-check via send_outbound`.
   - `pacer.py`: paced answer/reaction emissions.
   - `discord.py`/`whatsapp.py`: grep-confirmed all outbound routes through `messaging.send_outbound`/`send_outbound_part`. Annotate with `# pause-check N/A: routes through send_outbound`.
4. **Audit method**: grep each file for `send_outbound`, `send_outbound_part`, or direct outbound INSERTs. Each site either passes a gate OR carries a `# pause-check N/A: <reason>` comment. STOP if structurally hard.
5. **Write path stays deferred**: no code writes to `user_bot_state.paused` in S2a. Document at the top of `system_state.py`.

## Phase 4: Observability, lint, tests, validation

### Step 14: Per-bot observability fields (`app/services/turn_context.py`, then enumerated emitter files)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `obs_fields(ctx_or_scope) -> dict` to `app/services/turn_context.py` returning `{"bot_id": ..., "topic_id": ..., "channel_id": ..., "binding_id": ...}` (None values filtered).
2. **Hot-path** `logger.*` calls pass `extra=obs_fields(ctx)`:
   - `inbound.py`, `agentic.py`, `messaging.py`, `scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `oob_check.py`, `turn_audit.py`.
3. **Adjacent emitters** with concrete line ranges:
   - `discord.py:107-110` (`send_text`), `:115-122` (`add_reaction`), `:129-130` (edit), `:137-138` (delete), `:153-156` (`send_typing`), `:411-421` (gateway message handler), `:450-478` (reaction handler), `:481-522` (`catch_up_recent_messages`). Where no `ctx` exists, build scope from local `bot_id`/`channel_id` or call `_resolve_scope` once at function top.
   - `whatsapp.py` (~150 lines): apply `extra=` to every `logger.*` site.
   - `hooks.py` (40 lines): `paused_for_user` (`:37`) logs `extra={"user_id": user_id, "bot_id": bot_id}` (uses the new kwarg from Step 13.2); `_default_check_oob` (`:13-28`) logs `extra={"user_id": recipient_id}` if it logs at all.
   - `withheld_reviews.py`: thread `bot_id`/`topic_id` from the caller (Step 10.3) into both the row INSERT (column write deferred to S2b) and any `logger.*` calls' `extra=`.
   For sites with **no scope context** (transport-only connection errors), skip and add `# obs N/A: transport-only`.
4. **`record_turn_event` callers** (`agentic.py`, `turn_audit.py`): `metadata` dict carries `bot_id`/`topic_id`/`channel_id`/`binding_id`. Function shape unchanged.
5. **Note** in `s2a_preflight.md`: dashboard panes are downstream config, out of scope.

### Step 15: Advisory linter for INSERTs (`scripts/lint_inserts.py`, CI workflow)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `scripts/lint_inserts.py` — argparse `--help`. Walks `app/` files; scans SQL string literals matching `INSERT INTO (messages|bot_turns|scheduled_jobs|feedback|bridge_candidates)`; flags missing `bot_id`/`topic_id` (and `dyad_id` for `bridge_candidates`).
2. **Same** scanner flags `INSERT INTO (memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds)` whose enclosing SQL string lacks an `INSERT INTO artifact_topics` clause (substring check, not structural).
3. **CI step**: `lint-inserts` runs `python scripts/lint_inserts.py || true` — exit always 0 in S2a. `# TODO(S2b): make blocking` comment in the script.
4. **Smoke** `python scripts/lint_inserts.py --help`. Output: `path:line: kind: missing column 'bot_id'`.

### Step 16: Tests + fixtures (`tests/_scope_helpers.py`, new `tests/test_s2a_*.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Do NOT edit** dirty test files. Create `tests/_scope_helpers.py` exposing:
   - `make_mediator_ctx(bot_spec=None, **overrides)` building a `TurnContext` with mediator defaults including `dyad_id`.
   - `make_resolved_scope(**overrides)` mock `ResolvedScope`.
   - **`StampingFakePool(FakePool)`** subclass — **substring matching only**, not structural SQL parsing. Pseudocode:
     ```python
     class StampingFakePool(FakePool):
         def __init__(self):
             super().__init__()
             self._artifacts: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
             self._artifact_topics: list[dict] = []

         async def fetchrow(self, sql, *args):
             # Substring detection — does NOT require exact CTE wording.
             for table in ("memories", "themes", "observations",
                           "watch_items", "distillations", "out_of_bounds"):
                 if f"INSERT INTO {table}" in sql:
                     row = {"id": uuid4(), "args": args}
                     self._artifacts.setdefault(table, []).append(row)
                     if "INSERT INTO artifact_topics" in sql:
                         self._artifact_topics.append({"artifact_table": table,
                                                       "artifact_id": row["id"]})
                     return row
             return await super().fetchrow(sql, *args)
     ```
2. **Add** new test files:
   - `tests/test_s2a_stamping.py` — verifies `messages`/`bot_turns`/`scheduled_jobs`/`feedback`/`bridge_candidates` stamps including `bot_spec_version`/`hot_context_builder_version`/`tool_schema_version` (sha1, deterministic across two process runs) and `bridge_candidates.dyad_id`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py` — every artifact write produces an `artifact_topics` row via `StampingFakePool`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py` — `(user_id, bot_id)` and `(user_id, None)` keys flush correctly.
   - `tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py` — EXISTS query includes `(bot_id = $ OR bot_id IS NULL)`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py` — `user_bot_state.paused=TRUE` withholds at messaging, scheduler dispatch, and at least one re-emission site (`withheld_reviews` or `pacer`).
   - `tests/test_s2a_scheduler_dispatch_bot_id.py` — instrumented handler asserts `job["bot_id"]` populated; checks the claim-query RETURNING list contains `bot_id, topic_id`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_discord_id_helper.py` — `discord_bot_user_id()` returns env var when set, falls back to decoded token, returns None when both empty. Confirms `_decode_discord_user_id` parity with the S1 seeder.
   - `tests/test_s2a_lint_inserts.py` — invokes `scripts/lint_inserts.py` against fixture strings.

### Step 17: Validate + branch hygiene
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `pytest -q` — green. If any S1-passing test fails, STOP.
2. **Re-run** `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1`; diff against `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/`. Note drift in the PR description.
3. **Branch hygiene**: `git diff --stat <s1-tip>..HEAD -- <each dirty file>`. The 8 non-`write_tools.py` dirty files show zero changes; `write_tools.py` shows only additive INSERT-localized changes. `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md` not committed.
4. **Confirm** each `write_tools.py` commit is independently atomic.

## Execution Order
1. **Phase 0** first — eval baseline in the `d4c2a7c` worktree before any code change.
2. **Phase 1** (Steps 3–6) — discord_id helper, startup hook, `TurnContext.dyad_id`, routing wired into inbound, scope on every `TurnContext` site, `bot_turns` stamped.
3. **Phase 2** (Steps 7–10) — outbound, scheduler (claim query + handlers + seeds), artifacts (one commit per CTE rewrite/extend), bridge/feedback/withheld_reviews kwargs.
4. **Phase 3** (Steps 11–13) — coalescer dual-key, suppression dual-key, pause read-path with broader survey + gateway audit + `hooks.paused_for_user(bot_id=...)`.
5. **Phase 4** (Steps 14–17) — observability fan-out, linter, tests, final run + branch hygiene.

## Validation Order
1. After Step 2: `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` populated; worktree removed; `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py --help` parses.
2. After Step 3: `pytest tests/test_s2a_discord_id_helper.py -q` (or focused new test); the seeder script's import still works (`python scripts/seed_channels.py --help` if argparse, otherwise import-only smoke).
3. After Step 6: `pytest tests/test_agentic.py tests/test_inbound_source.py tests/test_routing.py -q`.
4. After Step 9: `pytest tests/test_s2a_stamping.py tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py -q`.
5. After Step 10: bridge / feedback / withheld_reviews tests pass.
6. After Step 11: `pytest tests/test_debouncer.py tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py -q`.
7. After Step 12: `pytest tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py -q`.
8. After Step 13: `pytest tests/test_send_outbound.py tests/test_pause_resume.py tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py -q`.
9. After Step 15: `python scripts/lint_inserts.py app/` — iterate until empty for new code.
10. After Step 16: full `pytest -q` green.
11. Final: `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1` diff vs baseline; Step 17 branch hygiene verified.

    "idea": "# Sprint 2a \u2014 Stamp insert sites, [REDACTED], observability, eval baseline\n\n## Orchestration\n\n**Working dir.** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas` on a feature branch (you create one as the last task). Legacy reader paths are retired in S2b, not here.\n2. **Lint is advisory in S2a, blocking in S2b.** Warn on missing `bot_id`/`topic_id` in new INSERTs; don't fail CI yet.\n3. **Eval baseline storage**: under `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` with one fixture per scenario. That's S2b.\n\n## Sprint 2a \u2014 \u00a716.2 verbatim\n\n**Goal.** Insert sites write the new columns; coalescer and newer-inbound suppression are rekeyed with a dual-key transition; per-bot observability comes online; pre-S3 eval baseline captured. Mediator behavior unchanged. **No NOT NULL.**\n\n**Pre-flight context.**\n\n- Confirm S1 backfill held \u2014 query `SELECT count(*) FROM mediator.artifact_topics` (must be 235 from S1; no new writes yet).\n- Exhaustive grep sweep of insert sites, broader than the obvious ones: `inbound.py:161`, `messaging.py:55, 84`, `agentic.py:483`, `agentic.py:577` (deferred jobs), `scheduled_jobs.py:216` (scheduler seeds), `write_tools.py:1367` (scheduled-task writes), every `INSERT INTO memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds` site. Stamp insert sites:\n   - `inbound.py:161` \u2014 `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `channel_id`.\n   - `messaging.py:55, 84` \u2014 same on outbound.\n   - `agentic.py:483` \u2014 `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `bot_spec_version` (hash of `BotSpec` repr), `hot_context_builder_version`, `tool_schema_version` (hash of `tool_schemas.py`).\n   - `agentic.py:577` deferred jobs and `scheduled_jobs.py:216` scheduler seeds \u2014 stamp `bot_id`, `topic_id`.\n   - `write_tools.py:1367` scheduled-task writes \u2014 same.\n   - Every artifact write in `write_tools.py`: stamp `recorded_by_bot_id` AND create matching `artifact_topics` row(s) for the bot's primary topic in the same transaction.\n3. **Dual-key burst coalescer** (`app/services/debouncer.py`). In-memory dicts keyed by `user_id` today; new code services BOTH `(user_id)` and `(user_id, bot_id)` keys during this sprint. Bursts in-flight at deploy flush under old key; new bursts use the composite key. Drop the legacy reader in S2b.\n4. **Dual-key newer-inbound suppression**:\n   - `agentic.py:612` (final-send suppression).\n   - `read_tools.py:166` (incremental-send suppression).\n   - Filter: `(bot_id = ctx.bot_id OR bot_id IS NULL)` during transition \u2014 the `IS NULL` half catches legacy boundary rows written before the stamping deploy.\n5. Scheduled-job dispatcher (`scheduled_jobs.py`) reads `bot_id` from row. No-op with one bot but the code path is live.\n6. **Per-bot observability**: every log line and structured event in inbound, agentic, messaging, scheduler, OOB check carries `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `channel_id`, `binding_id`. Dashboards split by bot (even though only one bot exists yet \u2014 the panes are there for S5).\n7. **Per-(user, bot) pause read path**: outbound and scheduler consult `user_bot_state.paused` in addition to `system_state.is_paused`. Write path for per-(user, bot) pause stays deferred. Global pause remains the kill switch.\n8. **Eval baseline**: run the existing mediator eval suite against current prod-like state and snapshot results. This is the pre-S3 regression reference.\n9. Tests/fixtures take a `bot_spec` argument (default mediator).\n10. **Lint**: CI warns (does NOT fail) on any new artifact INSERT outside a transaction that also creates the `artifact_topics` row, and on any new `INSERT INTO messages|bot_turns|scheduled_jobs|feedback` missing `bot_id`/`topic_id`.\n\nDependencies: (1) gates (2\u20137); (8) runs against pre-stamp prod, so day 1.\n\n**Definition of done.**\n\n- All insert sites stamp the new columns. Coalescer + suppression rekeyed with dual-key transition live. Per-bot observability live. Per-(user, bot) pause read-path live (write-path deferred). Eval baseline captured. **No NOT NULL constraints yet.**\n- Full pytest green, no regressions.\n- Every new INSERT statement either includes `bot_id`/`topic_id` or is flagged by the lint (advisory).\n\n## S1 lessons the executor must honor\n\n1. **Before declaring any new script/CLI complete**: actually invoke it with `--help` (or equivalent entry point) to confirm it parses and basic argparse setup works. Pytest passing is necessary but not sufficient \u2014 pytest may bypass `main()`.\n2. **Any SQL fragment in a Python string**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` against the live schema, or sanity-check the aggregate/operator types against actual column types (e.g. `max(uuid)` doesn't exist in Postgres).\n3. **Any asyncpg connection** against the configured `DATABASE_URL`: pass `statement_cache_size=0` \u2014 Supabase transaction-mode pooler (port 6543) is what's configured. Without this flag, scripts crash on first prepared-statement reuse.\n4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs**: Python's `hash()` is salted per-process. Use `hashlib.sha1` for advisory-lock-id derivation or anything needing cross-run determinism.\n5. **Schema filters in validation queries**: production tables live in schema `mediator`, NOT `public`. `information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema = 'mediator'`.\n\n## Stop conditions\n\n- Any test that was passing on `main` starts failing \u2014 STOP, report.\n- Any work item's executor decides the design is wrong and tries to redesign \u2014 STOP, report.\n- Schema migration would touch existing rows destructively \u2014 STOP, report.\n- Any artifact INSERT path can't fit `artifact_topics` write in the same transaction \u2014 STOP, report.",

        Requirements:
        - Produce structured JSON only.
        - `tasks` must be an ordered array of task objects. Every task object must include:
          - `id`: short stable task ID like `T1`
          - `description`: concrete work item
          - `depends_on`: array of earlier task IDs or `[]`
          - `status`: always `"pending"` at finalize time
          - `executor_notes`: always `""` at finalize time
          - `reviewer_verdict`: always `""` at finalize time
          - `kind`: indicating the type of work. One of:
            - `code`: writes or modifies source files (executor must produce `files_changed`).
            - `test`: writes or modifies test files, or runs the test suite (executor must produce `files_changed` in tests/ OR `commands_run` containing pytest/test invocations).
            - `audit`: read-only investigation — grep, code review, schema inspection (executor evidence is `executor_notes` describing findings; no `files_changed` expected).
            - `research`: external research or non-code investigation (executor evidence is `executor_notes`).
            - `docs`: writes documentation files (executor must produce `files_changed`).
            If unsure, default to `code`.
        - `watch_items` must be an array of strings covering runtime risks, critique concerns, and assumptions to keep visible during execution.
        - `sense_checks` must be an array with one verification question per task. Every sense-check object must include:
          - `id`: short stable ID like `SC1`
          - `task_id`: the related task ID
          - `question`: reviewer verification question
          - `verdict`: always `""` at finalize time
        - `user_actions` must be an array of human-only setup or operational actions. Use IDs `U1`, `U2`, ... and include `description` plus `phase` (`before_execute` or `after_execute`). Use optional `blocks_task_ids` when an action blocks specific tasks, optional `rationale` when useful, and `requires_human_only_reason` ONLY when the user_action is the sole coverage for a plan step.
- Include ONLY actions that require a human outside the executor's repo-editing work: env vars or secrets, infra access such as cloud accounts or VPN, DB migrations the human must trigger, manual UI/UX smoke tests, deploys, and out-of-band approvals.
- Anything that touches code in the repo MUST be a task, not a user_action. Reading docs, editing files, running tests, and writing migration SQL are tasks. Negative example: writing the migration SQL is a task, not a user_action.
- Positive examples: `U1: Set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY in .env (before_execute)`; `U2: Manually smoke test the production deploy in the browser (after_execute)`.
        - `meta_commentary` must be a single string with execution guidance, gotchas, or judgment calls that help the executor succeed.
        - `validation` must be an object that self-checks plan coverage:
          - `plan_steps_covered`: enumerate EVERY step from the approved plan. For each step, provide a short `plan_step_summary` (the step's intent in one phrase) and `finalize_item_ids` (array of task IDs `T*` or user_action IDs `U*` that implement or cover it — a single plan step may map to multiple tasks and/or user_actions).
          - `orphan_tasks`: task IDs that do not correspond to any plan step. Normally empty. If non-empty, explain in `completeness_notes`.
          - `completeness_notes`: free-text explanation of any gaps, deviations, or deliberate omissions.
          - `coverage_complete`: set to `true` only if every plan step has at least one finalize task or user_action AND you have verified the mapping by reviewing each entry. Set to `false` if any plan step is missing coverage.
          - Example:
          ```json
          "validation": {
            "plan_steps_covered": [
              {"plan_step_summary": "Add retry logic to API client", "finalize_item_ids": ["T1", "T2"]},
              {"plan_step_summary": "Set required production secret", "finalize_item_ids": ["U1"]}
            ],
            "orphan_tasks": [],
            "completeness_notes": "All plan steps mapped to tasks.",
            "coverage_complete": true
          }
          ```
        - Preserve information that strong existing artifacts already capture well: execution ordering, watch-outs, reviewer checkpoints, and practical context.
        - The structured output should be self-contained: an executor reading only `finalize.json` should have everything needed to work.
        - Keep the task count proportional to the work. A simple 1-2 file fix should be 2 tasks: (1) apply the fix, (2) run tests. Do NOT create separate "inspect" or "read" tasks for simple changes — the executor can read and fix in one step. Only create more tasks when the work has genuinely independent stages.
        - - The FINAL task MUST always be to run tests and verify the changes work. If specific test IDs or commands are mentioned in the original task, include them. Otherwise, the executor should find and run the tests most relevant to the files changed. If any test fails, read the error, fix the code, and re-run until they pass. Do NOT create new test files — run the project's existing test suite. Additionally, the executor should write a short throwaway script that reproduces the specific bug described in the task, run it to confirm the fix works, then delete the script.

Execution order matters: Phase 0 (T1-T2) MUST land before any code change so the eval baseline is captured against S1 tip d4c2a7c, not against partially-stamped code. Phase 1 (T3-T6) gates Phase 2 (T7-T10) because TurnContext.dyad_id and the routing helpers must exist before insert sites can stamp them. T11 (coalescer) and T12 (suppression) can be done after T5/T6 in parallel with T7-T10 if helpful, but the linear order in the plan is safer.\\n\\nKey gotchas: (1) `write_tools.py` is in the dirty-files list — every edit is INSERT-localized, in its own atomic commit, verified with `git diff --stat`; the supersede_memory and revise_distillation CTEs are EXTENDED with a new arm (NEVER rewritten). (2) The discord reaction handler resolves scope via the BOT's address (`discord_bot_user_id()`), NEVER the reacting user. (3) `bot_spec_version`/`tool_schema_version`/`hot_context_builder_version` all use `hashlib.sha1` — Python's `hash()` is salted per-process and will break determinism (S1 lesson). (4) Line numbers in the plan may drift ±2 from the current file; use function/symbol names as authoritative anchors. (5) `hooks.paused_for_user` gets a `bot_id` kwarg that is intentionally dead in S2a — leave the `# TODO(S2b)` activation comment. (6) When updating `scripts/seed_channels.py` to import from the new shared `discord_id.py`, DELETE the local copies to avoid drift. (7) Eval baseline must be captured inside a git worktree pinned to `d4c2a7c` — the script doesn't exist at that commit, so it's written in the main tree first, copied into the worktree, executed there, output copied back, worktree removed.\\n\\nSTOP conditions are aggressive — surface them immediately rather than redesigning. The plan budgets each STOP signal as a real escalation: an artifact INSERT that can't fit `artifact_topics` in the same statement, a destructive migration, a test that was green on `main` going red, or any insert path the executor decides shouldn't follow the pattern. If any pause-gate site is structurally hard, STOP rather than refactoring.\\n\\nThe final task (T17) includes both `pytest -q` and a small throwaway end-to-end repro script that exercises the stamping path — write it, run it green, delete it. # Sprint 2a — Stamp insert sites, [REDACTED], observability, eval baseline

## Orchestration

**Working dir.** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas` on a feature branch (you create one as the last task). Do NOT modify the architecture doc at `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md` — read-only reference.

**Pre-existing dirty files** (out of scope; mtimes confirm they predate this sprint — do NOT touch them, do NOT include in any commit): `app/services/hot_context.py`, `app/services/prompts.py`, `app/services/time_context.py`, `app/services/tools/write_tools.py` (read-only for the new stamping work; you'll INSERT lines, do not edit the existing modifications), `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_hot_context.py`, `tests/test_time_context.py`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tool_schemas.py`, `migrations/0019_feedback_reaction_context.sql`. S1's files (everything new since branch `s1-foundation`) are on disk and committed — build on top of them.

**Profile (locked).** `--profile thoughtful --depth high --phase-model finalize=claude:high`. Reason: tier-3 cross-cutting hot-path code; finalize on Claude pre-empts the DeepSeek empty-payload failure mode we hit in S1.

**Locked decisions (do not relitigate during plan/critique):**
1. **Dual-key transition stays dual.** Coalescer and newer-inbound suppression service BOTH `(user_id)` and `(user_id, bot_id)` keys for the duration of S2a. Legacy reader paths are retired in S2b, not here.
2. **Lint is advisory in S2a, blocking in S2b.** Warn on missing `bot_id`/`topic_id` in new INSERTs; don't fail CI yet.
3. **Eval baseline storage**: under `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` with one fixture per scenario. Frozen `now_utc` and frozen DB snapshot.
4. **Transaction discipline**: every artifact INSERT must wrap its `artifact_topics` write in the same transaction. Orphan = bug.
5. **No NOT NULL** anywhere in this sprint. That's S2b.

## Sprint 2a — §16.2 verbatim

**Goal.** Insert sites write the new columns; coalescer and newer-inbound suppression are rekeyed with a dual-key transition; per-bot observability comes online; pre-S3 eval baseline captured. Mediator behavior unchanged. **No NOT NULL.**

**Pre-flight context.**

- Confirm S1 backfill held — query `SELECT count(*) FROM mediator.artifact_topics` (must be 235 from S1; no new writes yet).
- Exhaustive grep sweep of insert sites, broader than the obvious ones: `inbound.py:161`, `messaging.py:55, 84`, `agentic.py:483`, `agentic.py:577` (deferred jobs), `scheduled_jobs.py:216` (scheduler seeds), `write_tools.py:1367` (scheduled-task writes), every `INSERT INTO memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds` site. Checklist file local to the sprint.

**Work items, ordered.**

1. Wire `routing.py` into `inbound.py:23-180`. Populate `TurnContext` with real scope fields (still mediator-only defaults).
2. Stamp insert sites:
   - `inbound.py:161` — `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `channel_id`.
   - `messaging.py:55, 84` — same on outbound.
   - `agentic.py:483` — `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `bot_spec_version` (hash of `BotSpec` repr), `hot_context_builder_version`, `tool_schema_version` (hash of `tool_schemas.py`).
   - `agentic.py:577` deferred jobs and `scheduled_jobs.py:216` scheduler seeds — stamp `bot_id`, `topic_id`.
   - `write_tools.py:1367` scheduled-task writes — same.
   - Every artifact write in `write_tools.py`: stamp `recorded_by_bot_id` AND create matching `artifact_topics` row(s) for the bot's primary topic in the same transaction.
3. **Dual-key burst coalescer** (`app/services/debouncer.py`). In-memory dicts keyed by `user_id` today; new code services BOTH `(user_id)` and `(user_id, bot_id)` keys during this sprint. Bursts in-flight at deploy flush under old key; new bursts use the composite key. Drop the legacy reader in S2b.
4. **Dual-key newer-inbound suppression**:
   - `agentic.py:612` (final-send suppression).
   - `read_tools.py:166` (incremental-send suppression).
   - Filter: `(bot_id = ctx.bot_id OR bot_id IS NULL)` during transition — the `IS NULL` half catches legacy boundary rows written before the stamping deploy.
5. Scheduled-job dispatcher (`scheduled_jobs.py`) reads `bot_id` from row. No-op with one bot but the code path is live.
6. **Per-bot observability**: every log line and structured event in inbound, agentic, messaging, scheduler, OOB check carries `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `channel_id`, `binding_id`. Dashboards split by bot (even though only one bot exists yet — the panes are there for S5).
7. **Per-(user, bot) pause read path**: outbound and scheduler consult `user_bot_state.paused` in addition to `system_state.is_paused`. Write path for per-(user, bot) pause stays deferred. Global pause remains the kill switch.
8. **Eval baseline**: run the existing mediator eval suite against current prod-like state and snapshot results. This is the pre-S3 regression reference.
9. Tests/fixtures take a `bot_spec` argument (default mediator).
10. **Lint**: CI warns (does NOT fail) on any new artifact INSERT outside a transaction that also creates the `artifact_topics` row, and on any new `INSERT INTO messages|bot_turns|scheduled_jobs|feedback` missing `bot_id`/`topic_id`.

Dependencies: (1) gates (2–7); (8) runs against pre-stamp prod, so day 1.

**Definition of done.**

- All insert sites stamp the new columns. Coalescer + suppression rekeyed with dual-key transition live. Per-bot observability live. Per-(user, bot) pause read-path live (write-path deferred). Eval baseline captured. **No NOT NULL constraints yet.**
- Full pytest green, no regressions.
- Every new INSERT statement either includes `bot_id`/`topic_id` or is flagged by the lint (advisory).

## S1 lessons the executor must honor

1. **Before declaring any new script/CLI complete**: actually invoke it with `--help` (or equivalent entry point) to confirm it parses and basic argparse setup works. Pytest passing is necessary but not sufficient — pytest may bypass `main()`.
2. **Any SQL fragment in a Python string**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` against the live schema, or sanity-check the aggregate/operator types against actual column types (e.g. `max(uuid)` doesn't exist in Postgres).
3. **Any asyncpg connection** against the configured `DATABASE_URL`: pass `statement_cache_size=0` — Supabase transaction-mode pooler (port 6543) is what's configured. Without this flag, scripts crash on first prepared-statement reuse.
4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs**: Python's `hash()` is salted per-process. Use `hashlib.sha1` for advisory-lock-id derivation or anything needing cross-run determinism.
5. **Schema filters in validation queries**: production tables live in schema `mediator`, NOT `public`. `information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema = 'mediator'`.

## Stop conditions

- Any test that was passing on `main` starts failing — STOP, report.
- Any work item's executor decides the design is wrong and tries to redesign — STOP, report.
- Schema migration would touch existing rows destructively — STOP, report.
- Any artifact INSERT path can't fit `artifact_topics` write in the same transaction — STOP, report.

User notes and answers:
- T2 (eval baseline capture) is BLOCKED by Supabase transaction-mode pooler limitation — SET search_path does not persist across pooled connections. This is a known infrastructure limit, NOT a sprint deliverable bug. DEFER T2: skip it for now, capture eval baseline manually at end of sprint using port 5432 session-mode OR defer entirely to pre-S3. PROCEED with T6-T17 (the actual stamping/rekeying/observability work) which is the core S2a deliverable and is independent of T2. Mark T2 as deferred in plan; do not retry; do not block batches on it.

        Approved plan:
        # Implementation Plan: S2a — Stamp insert sites, [REDACTED], observability, eval baseline

## Overview

S1 landed the additive schema (topics, bots, dyads, bindings, channels, identities, `artifact_topics`, `topic_status`, `user_bot_state`, nullable scope columns on `messages`/`bot_turns`/`scheduled_jobs`/`feedback`/`bridge_candidates`/artifact tables) and skeleton helpers (`routing.py`, extended `TurnContext` + `BotSpec`). S2a is the write-path pass.

**No root-cause re-think — the remaining 6 open precision flags are executor-precision details, not architectural problems.** v3 → v4 deltas close those last gaps:

- **correctness-1 / issue_hints / FLAG-016 (discord bot user id at runtime)**: `_decode_discord_user_id` lives in `scripts/seed_channels.py:55` (script-local) and is **not importable from `app/`**. Resolution: **promote** the helper to `app/services/discord_id.py` (new tiny module exposing `discord_bot_user_id() -> str | None`) that (a) reads `os.environ.get("DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID")` and (b) falls back to decoding `os.environ.get("DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN")` with the same base64url logic. Update `scripts/seed_channels.py` to import from the new module — keeps a single source of truth and avoids the `Settings.discord_bot_user_id` AttributeError. `app/config.py` does NOT need a new field for S2a.
- **correctness-2 (scheduler claim query)**: confirmed location — `app/services/scheduled_jobs.py:104-132` (`ScheduledJobs._claim_due_jobs`). The RETURNING list at lines 124-125 must add `sj.bot_id, sj.topic_id`. Dispatch is `scheduled_jobs.py:134` (`_dispatch`). All handlers live in `scheduled_job_handlers.py` (`handle_*`); grep-confirmed they take `job: dict[str, Any]`, no signature changes.
- **scope (withheld_reviews row stamps)**: `withheld_reviews` table is **out of scope for INSERT-column stamping in S2a** (not in the brief's table list). However the row INSERTs in `app/services/withheld_reviews.py:record_withheld_outbound_review` should accept `bot_id`/`topic_id` kwargs that get stored if/when the columns exist later. For S2a, thread the values through the function signature (no DB column change) so S2b can land the migration + lint rule without a second refactor. Document in `s2a_preflight.md`.
- **all_locations-1 (StampingFakePool fragility)**: simplified — the fake pool does **substring matching** only, not structural SQL parsing. Logic: if `"INSERT INTO " + <table>` appears AND `"INSERT INTO artifact_topics"` appears in the same SQL string, record both rows in the side dicts. Exact CTE wording is irrelevant.
- **all_locations-2 (hooks.py characterization)**: confirmed — `app/services/hooks.py` is 40 lines total. Only relevant code: `paused_for_user(user_id)` at `:37` and the `check_oob` hook indirection (`:13-28`). The "when one is in flight" phrasing is replaced with a concrete rule: **`paused_for_user` and `_default_check_oob` log invocations carry `extra={"user_id": ..., "bot_id": ...}` when those values are available at the call site (always for `check_oob`; for `paused_for_user`, pass `bot_id` through as a new optional kwarg since this function is consulted by the per-bot pause read-path in Step 12 anyway).** No fuzzy guards.
- **callers (TurnContext.dyad_id field placement)**: `dyad_id: UUID | None = None` is **appended at the end of the existing optional-fields block** in `app/services/turn_context.py` (after `cross_topic_policy: str | None = None` at line 33, before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34). All existing positional-argument call sites (`tests/test_send_outbound.py:537,626,677,732`, `tests/test_llm_phase.py:73`, `tests/test_turn_plan.py:42,60,73`, `tests/test_evals_capture.py`) continue to work because they stop at the original positional fields and the new field has a default.

**Settled / not relitigated (per gate):**
- Dual-key coalescer transition stays dual (locked decision #1).
- `messages.channel_id` stays observability-only (no column, no migration).
- `bot_spec_version` = `sha1(repr(BotSpec))[:12]` intentionally tracks every dataclass field.
- Lint is advisory in S2a.
- Per-(user, bot) pause WRITE-path is deferred.

Hard guardrails (unchanged): no edits to the 9 pre-existing dirty files except the targeted-INSERT carve-out for `write_tools.py`; no edits to `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md`; no NOT NULL; no coalescer/suppression interface redesigns; STOP if any artifact INSERT can't fit `artifact_topics` in the same statement.

S1 lessons honored: `--help` smoke on every new CLI; `hashlib.sha1` (never `hash()`); `statement_cache_size=0` on new asyncpg connections; schema `mediator` for `information_schema`; SQL fragments sanity-checked against actual column types.

## Phase 1: Wire routing into inbound; surface scope on TurnContext

### Step 3: Promote `_decode_discord_user_id` to a shared module (`app/services/discord_id.py`, `scripts/seed_channels.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `app/services/discord_id.py`:
   ```python
   import base64, os, re

   _DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_RE = re.compile(r"^\d+$")

   def _decode_discord_user_id(token: str | None) -> str | None:
       if not token:
           return None
       try:
           segment = token.split(".", 1)[0]
           padded = segment + "=" * (-len(segment) % 4)
           decoded = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(padded).decode()
           return decoded if _DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_RE.match(decoded) else None
       except (ValueError, UnicodeDecodeError):
           return None

   def discord_bot_user_id() -> str | None:
       explicit = os.environ.get("DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID")
       if explicit and _DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_RE.match(explicit):
           return explicit
       return _decode_discord_user_id(os.environ.get("DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN"))
   ```
2. **Update** `scripts/seed_channels.py:55-90` to `from app.services.discord_id import _decode_discord_user_id`. Single source of truth; the seeder behavior is unchanged.
3. **No changes** to `app/config.py` Settings (avoids adding a field that would ripple through Pydantic validators).

### Step 4: Async startup hook for the relationship topic id (`app/bots/registry.py`, `app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `populate_topic_ids_from_db(pool)` to `app/bots/registry.py`. Stores the UUID on a **module-level slot ONLY** — no `MEDIATOR_BOT` mutation, no `BotSpec` field:
   ```python
   _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID: UUID | None = None

   async def populate_topic_ids_from_db(pool):
       global _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID
       row = await pool.fetchrow("SELECT id FROM mediator.topics WHERE slug = 'relationship'")
       _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID = row["id"] if row else None

   def get_relationship_topic_id() -> UUID | None:
       return _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID
   ```
2. **Wire** the call into `app/main.py` startup right after `populate_mediator_spec_from_db` (both inside FastAPI lifespan).
3. **Fallback**: log a warning if the row is missing; the slot stays `None` (column is nullable per migration `0023`). Tests inject a UUID via `tests/_scope_helpers.py`.

This is append-only relative to the original positional fields — every existing test call site (e.g. `tests/test_send_outbound.py:537,626,677,732`, `tests/test_llm_phase.py:73`, `tests/test_turn_plan.py:42,60,73`) continues to work because they stop at the pre-S1 positional fields and the new field has a default.

Calls `resolve_bot` → `resolve_sender` → `resolve_binding`. On miss falls back to mediator defaults (`bot_id="mediator"`, `topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()`, others `None`).

### Step 9: Artifact writes + `artifact_topics` in same statement (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Large

> **Edit-safety contract for `write_tools.py`** (dirty file, INSERT-only carve-out):
> - Every change uses the `Edit` tool with a small unique `old_string` matching only the affected SQL fragment.
> - **Do not** delete or re-flow surrounding code, comments, or whitespace.
> - **Each `write_tools.py` change lands in its own commit** so accidental reflow is caught at review time.
> - After every edit, run `git diff --stat app/services/tools/write_tools.py` and confirm only additive changes appear at the targeted line range.
> - For existing multi-arm CTEs (`supersede_memory`, `revise_distillation`), **ADD a new arm** — never rewrite.

1. **Simple artifact INSERTs** (memories `:699`, themes `:764`, watch_items `:800`, observations `:878`, distillations `:939`, out_of_bounds `:1109`) rewrite from:
   ```sql
   INSERT INTO memories (...) VALUES (...) RETURNING id
   ```
   to:
   ```sql
   WITH new_artifact AS (
       INSERT INTO memories (..., recorded_by_bot_id) VALUES (..., $K) RETURNING id
   ), topic_link AS (
       INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status)
       SELECT 'memories', new_artifact.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new_artifact
   )
   SELECT id FROM new_artifact
   ```
   `recorded_by_bot_id = ctx.bot_id`, `topic_id = ctx.primary_topic_id`.
2. **`supersede_memory` (`:737-757`)** — existing multi-arm CTE. **ADD** a third arm `, topic_link AS (INSERT INTO artifact_topics ... SELECT 'memories', new.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new)`. Keep `WITH old AS (UPDATE memories ...)` verbatim.
3. **`revise_distillation` (`:1041-1092`)** — existing multi-arm CTE (`old`, `new`, `revised_old`). **ADD** a fourth arm `, topic_link AS (INSERT INTO artifact_topics ... SELECT 'distillations', new.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new)`. Keep `revised_old` and the trailing `JOIN`/`SELECT`.
4. **STOP** and report if any artifact INSERT can't fit `artifact_topics` in the same statement. `_schedule_context_job` follow-ups on `watch_items`/`out_of_bounds` are intentionally separate (artifact + topic link atomic; scheduled-job side-effect outside) — DO NOT trigger STOP.

**Scope:** Small

**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `obs_fields(ctx_or_scope) -> dict` to `app/services/turn_context.py` returning `{"bot_id": ..., "topic_id": ..., "channel_id": ..., "binding_id": ...}` (None values filtered).
2. **Hot-path** `logger.*` calls pass `extra=obs_fields(ctx)`:
   - `inbound.py`, `agentic.py`, `messaging.py`, `scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `oob_check.py`, `turn_audit.py`.
3. **Adjacent emitters** with concrete line ranges:
   - `discord.py:107-110` (`send_text`), `:115-122` (`add_reaction`), `:129-130` (edit), `:137-138` (delete), `:153-156` (`send_typing`), `:411-421` (gateway message handler), `:450-478` (reaction handler), `:481-522` (`catch_up_recent_messages`). Where no `ctx` exists, build scope from local `bot_id`/`channel_id` or call `_resolve_scope` once at function top.
   - `whatsapp.py` (~150 lines): apply `extra=` to every `logger.*` site.
   - `hooks.py` (40 lines): `paused_for_user` (`:37`) logs `extra={"user_id": user_id, "bot_id": bot_id}` (uses the new kwarg from Step 13.2); `_default_check_oob` (`:13-28`) logs `extra={"user_id": recipient_id}` if it logs at all.
   - `withheld_reviews.py`: thread `bot_id`/`topic_id` from the caller (Step 10.3) into both the row INSERT (column write deferred to S2b) and any `logger.*` calls' `extra=`.
   For sites with **no scope context** (transport-only connection errors), skip and add `# obs N/A: transport-only`.
4. **`record_turn_event` callers** (`agentic.py`, `turn_audit.py`): `metadata` dict carries `bot_id`/`topic_id`/`channel_id`/`binding_id`. Function shape unchanged.
5. **Note** in `s2a_preflight.md`: dashboard panes are downstream config, out of scope.

### Step 15: Advisory linter for INSERTs (`scripts/lint_inserts.py`, CI workflow)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `scripts/lint_inserts.py` — argparse `--help`. Walks `app/` files; scans SQL string literals matching `INSERT INTO (messages|bot_turns|scheduled_jobs|feedback|bridge_candidates)`; flags missing `bot_id`/`topic_id` (and `dyad_id` for `bridge_candidates`).
2. **Same** scanner flags `INSERT INTO (memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds)` whose enclosing SQL string lacks an `INSERT INTO artifact_topics` clause (substring check, not structural).
3. **CI step**: `lint-inserts` runs `python scripts/lint_inserts.py || true` — exit always 0 in S2a. `# TODO(S2b): make blocking` comment in the script.
4. **Smoke** `python scripts/lint_inserts.py --help`. Output: `path:line: kind: missing column 'bot_id'`.

### Step 16: Tests + fixtures (`tests/_scope_helpers.py`, new `tests/test_s2a_*.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Do NOT edit** dirty test files. Create `tests/_scope_helpers.py` exposing:
   - `make_mediator_ctx(bot_spec=None, **overrides)` building a `TurnContext` with mediator defaults including `dyad_id`.
   - `make_resolved_scope(**overrides)` mock `ResolvedScope`.
   - **`StampingFakePool(FakePool)`** subclass — **substring matching only**, not structural SQL parsing. Pseudocode:
     ```python
     class StampingFakePool(FakePool):
         def __init__(self):
             super().__init__()
             self._artifacts: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
             self._artifact_topics: list[dict] = []

         async def fetchrow(self, sql, *args):
             # Substring detection — does NOT require exact CTE wording.
             for table in ("memories", "themes", "observations",
                           "watch_items", "distillations", "out_of_bounds"):
                 if f"INSERT INTO {table}" in sql:
                     row = {"id": uuid4(), "args": args}
                     self._artifacts.setdefault(table, []).append(row)
                     if "INSERT INTO artifact_topics" in sql:
                         self._artifact_topics.append({"artifact_table": table,
                                                       "artifact_id": row["id"]})
                     return row
             return await super().fetchrow(sql, *args)
     ```
2. **Add** new test files:
   - `tests/test_s2a_stamping.py` — verifies `messages`/`bot_turns`/`scheduled_jobs`/`feedback`/`bridge_candidates` stamps including `bot_spec_version`/`hot_context_builder_version`/`tool_schema_version` (sha1, deterministic across two process runs) and `bridge_candidates.dyad_id`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py` — every artifact write produces an `artifact_topics` row via `StampingFakePool`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py` — `(user_id, bot_id)` and `(user_id, None)` keys flush correctly.
   - `tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py` — EXISTS query includes `(bot_id = $ OR bot_id IS NULL)`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py` — `user_bot_state.paused=TRUE` withholds at messaging, scheduler dispatch, and at least one re-emission site (`withheld_reviews` or `pacer`).
   - `tests/test_s2a_scheduler_dispatch_bot_id.py` — instrumented handler asserts `job["bot_id"]` populated; checks the claim-query RETURNING list contains `bot_id, topic_id`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_discord_id_helper.py` — `discord_bot_user_id()` returns env var when set, falls back to decoded token, returns None when both empty. Confirms `_decode_discord_user_id` parity with the S1 seeder.
   - `tests/test_s2a_lint_inserts.py` — invokes `scripts/lint_inserts.py` against fixture strings.

### Step 17: Validate + branch hygiene
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `pytest -q` — green. If any S1-passing test fails, STOP.
2. **Re-run** `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1`; diff against `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/`. Note drift in the PR description.
3. **Branch hygiene**: `git diff --stat <s1-tip>..HEAD -- <each dirty file>`. The 8 non-`write_tools.py` dirty files show zero changes; `write_tools.py` shows only additive INSERT-localized changes. `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md` not committed.
4. **Confirm** each `write_tools.py` commit is independently atomic.

## Execution Order
1. **Phase 0** first — eval baseline in the `d4c2a7c` worktree before any code change.
2. **Phase 1** (Steps 3–6) — discord_id helper, startup hook, `TurnContext.dyad_id`, routing wired into inbound, scope on every `TurnContext` site, `bot_turns` stamped.
3. **Phase 2** (Steps 7–10) — outbound, scheduler (claim query + handlers + seeds), artifacts (one commit per CTE rewrite/extend), bridge/feedback/withheld_reviews kwargs.
4. **Phase 3** (Steps 11–13) — coalescer dual-key, suppression dual-key, pause read-path with broader survey + gateway audit + `hooks.paused_for_user(bot_id=...)`.
5. **Phase 4** (Steps 14–17) — observability fan-out, linter, tests, final run + branch hygiene.

## Validation Order
1. After Step 2: `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` populated; worktree removed; `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py --help` parses.
2. After Step 3: `pytest tests/test_s2a_discord_id_helper.py -q` (or focused new test); the seeder script's import still works (`python scripts/seed_channels.py --help` if argparse, otherwise import-only smoke).
3. After Step 6: `pytest tests/test_agentic.py tests/test_inbound_source.py tests/test_routing.py -q`.
4. After Step 9: `pytest tests/test_s2a_stamping.py tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py -q`.
5. After Step 10: bridge / feedback / withheld_reviews tests pass.
6. After Step 11: `pytest tests/test_debouncer.py tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py -q`.
7. After Step 12: `pytest tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py -q`.
8. After Step 13: `pytest tests/test_send_outbound.py tests/test_pause_resume.py tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py -q`.
9. After Step 15: `python scripts/lint_inserts.py app/` — iterate until empty for new code.
10. After Step 16: full `pytest -q` green.
11. Final: `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1` diff vs baseline; Step 17 branch hygiene verified.

Reason: tier-3 cross-cutting hot-path code; finalize on Claude pre-empts the DeepSeek empty-payload failure mode we hit in S1.\n\n**Locked decisions (do not relitigate during plan/critique):**\n1. **Dual-key transition stays dual.** Coalescer and newer-inbound suppression service BOTH `(user_id)` and `(user_id, bot_id)` keys for the duration of S2a. Legacy reader paths are retired in S2b, not here.\n2. **Lint is advisory in S2a, blocking in S2b.** Warn on missing `bot_id`/`topic_id` in new INSERTs; don't fail CI yet.\n3. **Eval baseline storage**: under `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` with one fixture per scenario. Frozen `now_utc` and frozen DB snapshot.\n4. **Transaction discipline**: every artifact INSERT must wrap its `artifact_topics` write in the same transaction. That's S2b.\n\n## Sprint 2a \u2014 \u00a716.2 verbatim\n\n**Goal.** Insert sites write the new columns; coalescer and newer-inbound suppression are rekeyed with a dual-key transition; per-bot observability comes online; pre-S3 eval baseline captured. Mediator behavior unchanged. **No NOT NULL.**\n\n**Pre-flight context.**\n\n- Confirm S1 backfill held \u2014 query `SELECT count(*) FROM mediator.artifact_topics` (must be 235 from S1; no new writes yet).\n- Exhaustive grep sweep of insert sites, broader than the obvious ones: `inbound.py:161`, `messaging.py:55, 84`, `agentic.py:483`, `agentic.py:577` (deferred jobs), `scheduled_jobs.py:216` (scheduler seeds), `write_tools.py:1367` (scheduled-task writes), every `INSERT INTO memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds` site. Checklist file local to the sprint.\n\n**Work items, ordered.**\n\n1. Wire `routing.py` into `inbound.py:23-180`. Populate `TurnContext` with real scope fields (still mediator-only defaults).\n2. Stamp insert sites:\n   - `inbound.py:161` \u2014 `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `channel_id`.\n   - `messaging.py:55, 84` \u2014 same on outbound.\n   - `agentic.py:483` \u2014 `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `bot_spec_version` (hash of `BotSpec` repr), `hot_context_builder_version`, `tool_schema_version` (hash of `tool_schemas.py`).\n   - `agentic.py:577` deferred jobs and `scheduled_jobs.py:216` scheduler seeds \u2014 stamp `bot_id`, `topic_id`.\n   - `write_tools.py:1367` scheduled-task writes \u2014 same.\n   - Every artifact write in `write_tools.py`: stamp `recorded_by_bot_id` AND create matching `artifact_topics` row(s) for the bot's primary topic in the same transaction.\n3. **Dual-key burst coalescer** (`app/services/debouncer.py`). In-memory dicts keyed by `user_id` today; new code services BOTH `(user_id)` and `(user_id, bot_id)` keys during this sprint. Bursts in-flight at deploy flush under old key; new bursts use the composite key. Drop the legacy reader in S2b.\n4. **Dual-key newer-inbound suppression**:\n   - `agentic.py:612` (final-send suppression).\n   - `read_tools.py:166` (incremental-send suppression).\n   - Filter: `(bot_id = ctx.bot_id OR bot_id IS NULL)` during transition \u2014 the `IS NULL` half catches legacy boundary rows written before the stamping deploy.\n5. Scheduled-job dispatcher (`scheduled_jobs.py`) reads `bot_id` from row. No-op with one bot but the code path is live.\n6. **Per-bot observability**: every log line and structured event in inbound, agentic, messaging, scheduler, OOB check carries `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `channel_id`, `binding_id`. Dashboards split by bot (even though only one bot exists yet \u2014 the panes are there for S5).\n7. **Per-(user, bot) pause read path**: outbound and scheduler consult `user_bot_state.paused` in addition to `system_state.is_paused`. Write path for per-(user, bot) pause stays deferred. Global pause remains the kill switch.\n8. **Eval baseline**: run the existing mediator eval suite against current prod-like state and snapshot results. This is the pre-S3 regression reference.\n9. Tests/fixtures take a `bot_spec` argument (default mediator).\n10. **Lint**: CI warns (does NOT fail) on any new artifact INSERT outside a transaction that also creates the `artifact_topics` row, and on any new `INSERT INTO messages|bot_turns|scheduled_jobs|feedback` missing `bot_id`/`topic_id`.\n\nDependencies: (1) gates (2\u20137); (8) runs against pre-stamp prod, so day 1.\n\n**Definition of done.**\n\n- All insert sites stamp the new columns. Coalescer + suppression rekeyed with dual-key transition live. Per-bot observability live. Per-(user, bot) pause read-path live (write-path deferred). Eval baseline captured. **No NOT NULL constraints yet.**\n- Full pytest green, no regressions.\n- Every new INSERT statement either includes `bot_id`/`topic_id` or is flagged by the lint (advisory).\n\n## S1 lessons the executor must honor\n\n1. **Before declaring any new script/CLI complete**: actually invoke it with `--help` (or equivalent entry point) to confirm it parses and basic argparse setup works. Pytest passing is necessary but not sufficient \u2014 pytest may bypass `main()`.\n2. **Any SQL fragment in a Python string**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` against the live schema, or sanity-check the aggregate/operator types against actual column types (e.g. `max(uuid)` doesn't exist in Postgres).\n3. **Any asyncpg connection** against the configured `DATABASE_URL`: pass `statement_cache_size=0` \u2014 Supabase transaction-mode pooler (port 6543) is what's configured. Without this flag, scripts crash on first prepared-statement reuse.\n4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs**: Python's `hash()` is salted per-process. Use `hashlib.sha1` for advisory-lock-id derivation or anything needing cross-run determinism.\n5. **Schema filters in validation queries**: production tables live in schema `mediator`, NOT `public`. `information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema = 'mediator'`.\n\n## Stop conditions\n\n- Any test that was passing on `main` starts failing \u2014 STOP, report.\n- Any work item's executor decides the design is wrong and tries to redesign \u2014 STOP, report.\n- Schema migration would touch existing rows destructively \u2014 STOP, report.\n- Any artifact INSERT path can't fit `artifact_topics` write in the same transaction \u2014 STOP, report.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
        [
  "[DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)",
  "[DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)",
  "[DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)",
  "[DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)",

        Requirements:
        - Produce structured JSON only.
        - `tasks` must be an ordered array of task objects. Every task object must include:
          - `id`: short stable task ID like `T1`
          - `description`: concrete work item
          - `depends_on`: array of earlier task IDs or `[]`
          - `status`: always `"pending"` at finalize time
          - `executor_notes`: always `""` at finalize time
          - `reviewer_verdict`: always `""` at finalize time
          - `kind`: indicating the type of work. One of:
            - `code`: writes or modifies source files (executor must produce `files_changed`).
            - `test`: writes or modifies test files, or runs the test suite (executor must produce `files_changed` in tests/ OR `commands_run` containing pytest/test invocations).
            - `audit`: read-only investigation — grep, code review, schema inspection (executor evidence is `executor_notes` describing findings; no `files_changed` expected).
            - `research`: external research or non-code investigation (executor evidence is `executor_notes`).
            - `docs`: writes documentation files (executor must produce `files_changed`).
            If unsure, default to `code`.
        - `watch_items` must be an array of strings covering runtime risks, critique concerns, and assumptions to keep visible during execution.
        - `sense_checks` must be an array with one verification question per task. Every sense-check object must include:
          - `id`: short stable ID like `SC1`
          - `task_id`: the related task ID
          - `question`: reviewer verification question
          - `verdict`: always `""` at finalize time
        - `user_actions` must be an array of human-only setup or operational actions. Use IDs `U1`, `U2`, ... and include `description` plus `phase` (`before_execute` or `after_execute`). Use optional `blocks_task_ids` when an action blocks specific tasks, optional `rationale` when useful, and `requires_human_only_reason` ONLY when the user_action is the sole coverage for a plan step.
- Include ONLY actions that require a human outside the executor's repo-editing work: env vars or secrets, infra access such as cloud accounts or VPN, DB migrations the human must trigger, manual UI/UX smoke tests, deploys, and out-of-band approvals.
- Anything that touches code in the repo MUST be a task, not a user_action. Reading docs, editing files, running tests, and writing migration SQL are tasks. Negative example: writing the migration SQL is a task, not a user_action.
- Positive examples: `U1: Set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY in .env (before_execute)`; `U2: Manually smoke test the production deploy in the browser (after_execute)`.
        - `meta_commentary` must be a single string with execution guidance, gotchas, or judgment calls that help the executor succeed.
        - `validation` must be an object that self-checks plan coverage:
          - `plan_steps_covered`: enumerate EVERY step from the approved plan. For each step, provide a short `plan_step_summary` (the step's intent in one phrase) and `finalize_item_ids` (array of task IDs `T*` or user_action IDs `U*` that implement or cover it — a single plan step may map to multiple tasks and/or user_actions).
          - `orphan_tasks`: task IDs that do not correspond to any plan step. Normally empty. If non-empty, explain in `completeness_notes`.
          - `completeness_notes`: free-text explanation of any gaps, deviations, or deliberate omissions.
          - `coverage_complete`: set to `true` only if every plan step has at least one finalize task or user_action AND you have verified the mapping by reviewing each entry. Set to `false` if any plan step is missing coverage.
          - Example:
          ```json
          "validation": {
            "plan_steps_covered": [
              {"plan_step_summary": "Add retry logic to API client", "finalize_item_ids": ["T1", "T2"]},
              {"plan_step_summary": "Set required production secret", "finalize_item_ids": ["U1"]}
            ],
            "orphan_tasks": [],
            "completeness_notes": "All plan steps mapped to tasks.",
            "coverage_complete": true
          }
          ```
        - Preserve information that strong existing artifacts already capture well: execution ordering, watch-outs, reviewer checkpoints, and practical context.
        - The structured output should be self-contained: an executor reading only `finalize.json` should have everything needed to work.
        - Keep the task count proportional to the work. A simple 1-2 file fix should be 2 tasks: (1) apply the fix, (2) run tests. Do NOT create separate "inspect" or "read" tasks for simple changes — the executor can read and fix in one step. Only create more tasks when the work has genuinely independent stages.
        - - The FINAL task MUST always be to run tests and verify the changes work. If specific test IDs or commands are mentioned in the original task, include them. Otherwise, the executor should find and run the tests most relevant to the files changed. If any test fails, read the error, fix the code, and re-run until they pass. Do NOT create new test files — run the project's existing test suite. Additionally, the executor should write a short throwaway script that reproduces the specific bug described in the task, run it to confirm the fix works, then delete the script.
